# TDD Evidence

## Planning checkpoint — 2026-08-13

No behavior changed and no tests ran for this OpenSpec-only planning commit. Core PRs #665–#671 are future adjudicated benchmark cases, not evidence that this change is implemented.

## Dependency amendment — 2026-08-19

The approved exact-core target changed from the unavailable planned 0.56.0 identity to existing immutable lightweight tag `v0.55.1`, full commit `b1e517e60e669eaba15a18ecfa83ef5a9df65276`, full tree `47984be5434d7ae65ed6908bf525a32053290337`, and strict `===0.55.1`. Live tag lookup and local commit/tree inspection established those identities. No test or source evidence is claimed by this planning amendment.

## Core-interface and capsule-composition amendment — 2026-08-21

Fresh core refs established `origin/dev@e3a20f20df440dff49f8c6d1f73375451bea1d8c`, `origin/main@b1e517e60e669eaba15a18ecfa83ef5a9df65276`, and immutable `v0.55.1` tree `47984be5434d7ae65ed6908bf525a32053290337`. A path-limited byte comparison of `module_discovery.py`, `module_installer.py`, and `module_package.py` found no difference between `origin/dev` and `v0.55.1`. The tag already contains `DiscoveredModule`, canonical user/marketplace roots, `.specfact-registry-id`, `.specfact-install-verified-checksum`, package-integrity parsing, bundled-key lookup, and `verify_module_artifact`. The earlier contract incorrectly assumed those records arrived as one aggregate loader DTO; no such later unpublished interface exists.

The approved amendment replaces that assumption with derived `core-v0.55.1-installed-module-handoff-v1`, separates workflow-attested `verified-candidate-module-payload-v1` from released official provenance, and distinguishes the immutable OCI base-root identity from generated `sealed-bootstrap-v2` plus `capsule-composite-identity-v1`. The earlier task 3.12e/3.12f green evidence proves descriptor-safe copying only and is not reused as evidence for this amended handoff/bootstrap contract. New named tests, collected selectors, checkpoint projection/digest, actual failing evidence, implementation, and passing evidence are required before either task returns to green.

## Release readiness evidence — 2026-08-21

### Exact core 0.55.1 handoff matrix

Fresh isolated CPython 3.11.15, 3.12.13, and 3.13.14 environments installed the immutable core tag from detached commit `b1e517e60e669eaba15a18ecfa83ef5a9df65276` / tree `47984be5434d7ae65ed6908bf525a32053290337`. Each environment reported core package version `0.55.1` and passed the schema 1.6 end-to-end, installed-handoff derivation, and candidate-payload identity selectors (`3 passed` per interpreter). `===0.55.1` accepted only `0.55.1`; it rejected `0.55.1+vendor`, while ordinary `==0.55.1` admitted that local alias and is therefore not release-authoritative.

The core trust surfaces required by the amended boundary are present in the tag. `module_discovery.py`, `module_installer.py`, and `module_package.py` are byte-identical between immutable v0.55.1 and the freshly fetched current core `origin/dev` observed during readiness validation. C14 derives a module-owned handoff from those existing granular records; it does not depend on an unpublished later core implementation.

### Task 4.2 adjudicated benchmark

Live GitHub bodies and states for core #665–#671 were refreshed on 2026-08-21. The retained #665 release corpus explicitly requires an exact range, unique merge base, changed-test/policy coverage, symmetric evidence, independent verdicts, and UNKNOWN for unresolved facts; #666–#671 remain historical remediation/forensic inputs rather than reusable proof.

Command: `hatch run pytest -q` over the eight exact selectors for staged-versus-unstaged index content, positional downgrade rejection, candidate-policy self-authorization rejection, policy-only range UNKNOWN, advanced-base-tip merge-base selection, pure-rename continuity, off-added-line introduced blockers, and head-config false-green prevention.

Outcome: `8 passed`, `0 failed` on Python 3.12.13. Task decision: `GREEN`.

### Task 4.3 release metadata

After the exact-core matrix passed, the permitted feature-branch release metadata was set to module version `0.49.0` and exact `core_compatibility: '===0.55.1'`; the changelog records the additive C14 range/differential/schema 1.6 release. The focused manifest, exact-core workflow, and schema consumer tests passed (`12 passed`, `2 deselected`). Archives, checksums, signatures, sidecars, and `registry/index.json` remain untouched for canonical post-merge generation. Final Linux cache-miss/cache-hit capsule and empty-Bubblewrap authority remains pending protected CI and canonical publication evidence; task 4.3 is not yet complete.

### Final feature-branch gates — 2026-08-21

After clean-code remediation, the candidate module's full local review completed in 39.11 seconds with `138 findings (0 blocking)`. The preceding runs reduced the blocker count from 40 to 14, then 1, then 0 without weakening any detector or accepted C14 behavior. Focused regression validation after the refactor passed `191` tests. The retained non-blocking review diagnostics are explicit pre-merge exceptions: `79` advisory contract-coverage findings across the frozen public C14 model/API surface; structural single-owner file-size/complexity diagnostics required by the closed C14 source allowlist; targeted local coverage warnings whose Linux capsule/cache/Bubblewrap paths are protected-CI owned; and one CrossHair environment error caused by its subprocess lacking `beartype`, while the canonical contract gate itself passed. Adding post-checkpoint decorators, splitting the mandated owner files, or pretending that macOS supplied Linux namespace authority would alter the accepted contract or evidence boundary. These exceptions grant no `pr_range` or Linux authority and remain visible in the JSON evidence.

The final checksum-only dev-PR bridge set `specfact-code-review` `0.49.0` to `sha256:bc205355b32388d968ef30919bd57f30d147f46472c9099e8c19fc8f8b5d5470`; `core_compatibility` is exactly `===0.55.1`, and the registry remains untouched. Filesystem signature/version-bump verification accepted all seven manifests with the local missing-public-key allowance. `git diff --check`, format, type-check (`0` errors), Ruff/Pylint (`10.00/10`), YAML, bundle imports, strict OpenSpec, and contract tests (`28 passed`) all passed. Both `smart-test` and the complete test gate passed all `1344` tests; the only warnings were two third-party `lark` deprecations.

### Post-merge release-gate remediation checkpoint

On 2026-08-23, issue #416 remained open, assigned to `djm81`, labelled `enhancement`, `codebase`, `openspec`, and `change-proposal`, attached to the SpecFact CLI project in `In Progress`, and linked in its body to parent #163 / epic #162. The user explicitly authorized this corrective continuation after PR #418 and the subsequent publication-chain PRs merged. The current `origin/dev` base was `e4f5c90ac246990a5156aea2ca2a842ac2e9d2d7`; the remediation remains on the isolated `bugfix/c14-release-blockers` worktree and is bounded to five confirmed release blockers already covered by the accepted C14 requirements. No paired core implementation or additional review finding is admitted into this checkpoint.

The pre-test inspection confirmed: capsule reports omit `suppression_catalog_digest`; immutable analysis expands from the complete snapshot rather than `selected_paths`; generated pytest/Coverage outputs target the unmounted `/opt/specfact/temporary`; the manifest's project-runtime schema digest does not equal the raw resource SHA-256 `8a0f825412f7081d4859d8e39b62d4b0b7b9536a8ba7ec7c1a5067e37bcffc15`; and the changelog still names the superseded `0.49.0` release identity. Focused red and green command evidence follows after the tests run.

Focused red command `hatch run python -m pytest -q` over the five new selectors collected all five tests and failed all five. The observed failures were exact: report digest `None` versus `sha256:32346a8a0848bc024b1330c37ab5bdcf12f092460cce904ebbab831f6d276375`; catalog drift still reported `PASS`; both snapshots analyzed `src/unrelated_blocker.py` in addition to `src/changed.py`; both generated configs used `/opt/specfact/temporary`; and the declared schema digest `sha256:006338a430d3b11be510fe10e2998245e00554ebe05fc628c2c4c0217f95a725` differed from the raw-byte digest `sha256:8a0f825412f7081d4859d8e39b62d4b0b7b9536a8ba7ec7c1a5067e37bcffc15`. This is the failing-before evidence for tasks 5.1–5.4.

The same five selectors then passed (`5 passed`), and the affected runner/differential/manifest surface passed `354` tests on the local controller. The complete suite passed `1568` tests while locally separating its two immutable-environment selectors: the exact core `0.55.1` consumer selector passed against local tag `v0.55.1` / commit `b1e517e60e669eaba15a18ecfa83ef5a9df65276`, and the sealed capsule-lock selector passed on supported CPython `3.12.13`; together all `1570` collected tests passed. The smart-test run likewise passed `1569` tests on CPython 3.14 and its sole unsupported-ABI capsule selector passed separately on CPython 3.12. Contract-first verification passed `28` tests. Format, Ruff, basedpyright (`0 errors, 0 warnings, 0 notes`), and Pylint (`10.00/10`) passed. Strict OpenSpec, seven manifest/checksum/version verifications, registry consistency, bundle imports, and `git diff --check` passed. The checksum-only signer advanced `specfact-code-review` from `0.49.46` to `0.49.47`, set checksum `sha256:9147d7b7324e66293378e8cb9c0f3033240e15e2c97dd995ba86fdd19a5f1f1b`, and retained exact core compatibility `===0.55.1`. GitHub's Module Signature Hardening workflow then added the signature-only commit `9b5bd16eb4f478a03871d6c3673f46f27609b855`; strict verification of all seven manifests with the exact core `0.55.1` public key passed. Subsequent corrective payload commits require the same workflow and strict verification on the resulting PR head.

Post-PR review added two focused red selectors. A jointly drifted catalog plus consumer matrix raised `AttributeError` because no independent-authority activation seam existed, and empty pytest/Coverage policies omitted `cache_dir` and `run:data_file`, leaving their defaults in the read-only snapshot. After binding checkpoint/profile identity to the frozen C14 digest, reading the suppression digest from the package manifest's authenticated resource entry, and projecting both default destinations under `/opt/specfact/tmp`, the two regressions and four nearest controls passed (`6 passed`).

The complete affected runner/differential/manifest surface then passed `356` tests on CPython 3.14.6. The repository-wide run passed `1571` tests there while deselecting the capsule-lock selector whose sealed environment supports CPython 3.11-3.13; that selector passed separately on CPython 3.12.13, covering all `1572` collected tests. The smart-test gate reproduced only that unsupported-controller split (`1571 passed`, one local CPython 3.14 failure), with the failed selector already green on CPython 3.12. Format, Ruff, basedpyright (`0 errors, 0 warnings, 0 notes`), Pylint (`10.00/10`), `28` contract-first tests, strict OpenSpec, seven manifest/registry checks, bundle imports, and `git diff --check` passed. The staged review gate returned `PASS_WITH_ADVISORY` with no changed-line blocker; its sole error was the same CPython 3.14 capsule-runtime outcome proven green on CPython 3.12. A fresh PR review then showed that redirecting defaults beneath `/opt/specfact/tmp/pytest` and `/opt/specfact/tmp/coverage` was insufficient unless those child roots existed before Bubblewrap launch. The focused selector failed red with both directories absent (`[(False, False)]` instead of `[(True, True)]`). The final scoped Codex review then identified a deletion-only applicability gap in the original selected-path/complete-pytest remediation. When a selected Python file existed in the base snapshot but not the head snapshot, the head-side analyzer file list was correctly empty, yet dispatch marked `targeted-pytest-coverage` `NOT_APPLICABLE` before using its independently prepared complete inventory. Focused selector `test_deleted_python_head_runs_complete_pytest_inventory` was authored first and failed because the snapshot runner had no selected-scope applicability input. Ruff, focused basedpyright (`0 errors, 0 warnings, 0 notes`), strict OpenSpec, and `git diff --check` passed. The unsigned final-byte checksum refresh retained unpublished version `0.49.47`, set `sha256:76ece874efbcb4ede8ea2cbb9bb19068edeb77b943cac1e56dd4a830f0f739be`, and verified all seven manifests against `origin/dev` with the exact core `0.55.1` public key; approval-time CI signing remains required. The final Codex review identified that task 6.1's isolated test contradicted the actual launch composition: `_execute_capsule_member` prepends its generated request root before every policy root, and the sandbox enumerates that combined tuple from zero. The signed-head Codex review then identified one further defect directly within task 6.2: staged primary or already-governed transitive Ruff/basedpyright entries with a symlink Git mode could fail during staged policy parsing before the populated index context reached `_unsafe_index_path`. ### PR #420 suppression-relocation remediation

On 2026-08-25, exact `origin/dev` head `3ef93ac4e4eda8762192faf95b83f67e074cbf9f` retained one newly surfaced, validated P2 finding from PR #420: suppression occurrences were paired only by rename-normalized path, family, normalized token, and ordinal, so moving one otherwise identical directive within the same file was misclassified as `unchanged_suppression_on_changed_file` `UNKNOWN` instead of an introduced blocking finding. This contradicts the accepted C14 contract that added, changed, or relocated registered directives are introduced blockers. Direct reproduction used base `# noqa: F401\nx=1\n` and head `x=1\n# noqa: F401\n`; the current result was `UNKNOWN`, with no introduced occurrence and the head line 2 occurrence marked unchanged. The control with an unrelated header inserted before the otherwise untouched directive also returned `UNKNOWN` quarantine with no introduced occurrence, which is the required legitimate line-shift behavior.

Issue [#416](https://github.com/nold-ai/specfact-cli-modules/issues/416) remains open under parent Feature #163 with labels `enhancement`, `codebase`, `openspec`, and `change-proposal`, project `SpecFact CLI`, status `In Progress`, and no declared blockers. The hierarchy cache was refreshed at `2026-08-25T19:58:01Z`; current GitHub issue state was read back afterward. The user explicitly authorized this isolated continuation after the finding and its proposed narrow boundary were disclosed. PR #429 and its canonical publication PR #430 were already merged to `dev`; no other PR #420 review finding is admitted here.

The pre-patch compatibility pass traced candidate-controlled Python comment placement through stdlib comment tokenization, occurrence pairing, `classify_suppression_delta`, and `_apply_suppression_delta`. The broken invariant is confined to the pairing step: a relocated occurrence can reuse a baseline ordinal without proving source-location continuity. Existing downstream `FAIL` propagation is correct. The narrow strategy is to reuse the existing bounded exact-line edit-cost machinery for changed source pairs, pair as unchanged only when the identical physical directive line is on a uniquely optimal correspondence, retain deterministic ordinal pairing for byte-identical sources including canonical one-to-one renames, and return `UNKNOWN` for correspondence uncertainty. Legitimate unrelated line insertion, identical rename, directive removal, catalog matching, and runner disposition remain unchanged. Alternate representations checked before patching were standalone and inline comments, duplicate identical directive lines, token changes, source-line edits, ordinary insertions, relocation, and exact renames; duplicates or correspondence bounds must fail closed rather than be guessed.

After the spec amendment and before production edits, the focused three-selector command collected the relocation regression plus the unrelated-line-shift and byte-identical-rename controls. The relocation selector failed exactly at the required boundary: actual status was `UNKNOWN` instead of expected `FAIL`. Both controls passed. This is the failing-before evidence for task 6.14.

The bounded implementation reuses the existing `source-line-correspondence-v1` edit matrices and resource limits. Byte-identical source pairs retain deterministic ordinal pairing. For changed source pairs, a suppression occurrence is unchanged only when its complete physical line is identical, unique on both sides, and forcing that pair preserves the global minimum edit cost while forbidding it raises the cost. A unique physical directive line without that proof is relocated or changed and introduced; invalid anchors, duplicate physical lines, or exceeded bounds remain quarantined `UNKNOWN`. The focused relocation and two legitimate controls passed (`3 passed`), and the complete differential unit surface passed (`93 passed`). Strict OpenSpec, focused Ruff format/check, and focused basedpyright (`0 errors, 0 warnings, 0 notes`) passed.

The separate candidate-patch challenge exercised an inline source-line change, an unrelated tail edit, duplicate identical directives, complete removal, and one added duplicate. The inline change was introduced `FAIL`; the unrelated tail edit retained the existing required quarantine; duplicate correspondence remained `UNKNOWN`; complete removal remained ordinary `PASS`; and the added duplicate remained visible while the pre-existing duplicate ambiguity kept the aggregate fail-closed. Direct callers and downstream suppression-to-review disposition were unchanged. No additional PR #420 finding was admitted. The candidate module is patch version `0.49.50`, retains exact core compatibility `===0.55.1`, has unsigned filesystem checksum `sha256:78b761709db05edec1c58233fc78097df1c7dca77deae776b159c28d2beee0e2`, and intentionally awaits the CI approval-time signature.

Final local gates on 2026-08-25 passed: format; full repository type check (`0 errors, 0 warnings, 0 notes`); Ruff; Pylint (`10.00/10`); strict C14 OpenSpec; seven manifests plus registry; bundle imports; filesystem checksum/version enforcement against `origin/dev`; publish precheck; `28` contract-first tests; and `git diff --check`. With the host CA bundle supplied to the Semgrep executable, independent smart and complete suites each passed `1613` of `1614` collected tests. Their sole failure was the pre-existing local CPython 3.14 capsule-lock case: the signed C14 analyzer runtime intentionally has environments only for protected CPython 3.11-3.13. An initial smart run without the host CA path also produced Semgrep trust-store setup failures; rerunning with `/etc/ssl/cert.pem` removed every one, confirming an environment input rather than a patch regression. The staged Code Review gate first passed `PASS_WITH_ADVISORY` with `15` warnings and `0` errors; one patch-caused parameter-count warning was corrected by grouping the two existing correspondence caches into one private state object. The repeated review passed with `14` inherited/tool-environment warnings and `0` errors, the focused differential surface remained `93 passed`, and repository lint remained clean. ### PR #420 final-head sandbox and mixed-suppression findings

On 2026-08-26, the refreshed PR #420 review contained two unresolved findings on current `origin/dev` head `18fd955af533e092ddcef96686904874b46898f0`. The P1 sandbox finding was validated by call-graph inspection: `validate_pre_namespace_objects` existed only as a unit-tested helper, while every real immutable analyzer launch proceeded from static descriptor validation directly to `subprocess.run`. This violated the accepted `linux-bwrap-v1` requirement that runtime pre-namespace mapping/open closure gate analyzer execution. The P2 aggregate finding was reproduced with one unchanged `# noqa: F401` in a changed file plus one introduced `# noqa: F821` mapped to Ruff. Forward finding order ended with member `UNKNOWN`, hiding the blocker; reversed order ended with member `FAIL` but omitted required uncertainty. Both defects are inside the existing C14 contract; no analyzer, catalog, detector, dependency, policy, or core-consumer scope was admitted.

After the existing spec scenarios were tightened and before production edits, the focused command collected five cases: same-descriptor launch routing, failed runtime pre-namespace validation, observed mapping/open closure, and both mixed-suppression finding orders. All five failed at the intended boundaries. The launch bypassed the new traced gate and still called `subprocess.run`; the runtime observer did not exist; forward suppression order returned `UNKNOWN` instead of `FAIL`; and reverse order lacked `required_unknown_reasons`. This is the failing-before evidence for task 6.31.

The bounded correction stops the verified descriptor launch at the Linux `PTRACE_TRACEME` exec boundary, derives the mapped-object and inherited-open closure from `/proc/<pid>`, and continues the process only after the existing validator returns `PASS`. Missing, ambiguous, substituted, dynamically mapped, or otherwise unclassified objects return `UNKNOWN`; unsupported platforms and multithreaded controller launches also fail closed. The suppression merge now keeps `FAIL` precedence while retaining sorted member-local `required_unknown_reasons`, so aggregate assurance remains `FAIL` and `has_unknown_required_evidence` remains true regardless of finding order.

The modified-scope review found two further applicable states inside those same invariants, without admitting a new finding family. An empty runtime observation previously passed the standalone validator, and a pre-existing unrecognized analyzer outcome could be overwritten by an introduced suppression failure without retaining the required uncertainty. Focused red regressions failed before each correction. The validator now requires exactly one observed executable, and the merge records `untrusted_preexisting_member_outcome`; a global suppression-catalog `UNKNOWN` also preserves an existing analyzer `FAIL`. The final focused surface passed `30` tests and the three affected unit files passed `370` of `371`; the sole failure is the pre-existing local CPython 3.14 capsule-lock environment gap. ### PR #438 signed-head review follow-up

The first signed-head review of PR #438 on 2026-08-26 raised one Codex P1 and two CodeRabbit data-integrity findings that remain inside tasks 6.31-6.32. Separately, a valid pre-existing `NOT_APPLICABLE` member was labelled `untrusted_preexisting_member_outcome` when global suppression uncertainty was merged, and missing-base reclassification used `model_copy(update=...)` to force every fixed finding to `blocking=true`, bypassing the canonical severity/lifecycle derivation. This is the failing-before evidence for task 6.34.

The bounded correction gives ptrace requests named constants, detaches immediately after successful runtime validation, and sends `SIGKILL` directly by PID before reaping any failed or timed-out stopped tracee. ### PR #420 final-head analyzer completion findings

On 2026-08-26, fresh review of PR #420 at signed `dev` head `67dc788d5ad86c8e570670063a92b66b0559f1d7` produced two Codex P1 findings inside the existing C14 analyzer contract. Projected basedpyright launches still appended positional source files after the generated `--project` argument, overriding the signed exact-include authority. Semgrep accepted parseable JSON without checking fatal process statuses or non-empty top-level structured errors, allowing incomplete execution to become PASS. The requested read-only review-agent pass over both changed runners confirmed those findings and found one additional instance of the same completion invariant: basedpyright fatal/configuration/argument exits were accepted whenever `generalDiagnostics` parsed. No rule, detector, policy, dependency, input-selection, or core-consumer change is admitted.

The spec scenarios were amended before test or production edits. Four focused regressions were then added and run together: projected basedpyright argv without positional sources, basedpyright fatal exit with parseable empty diagnostics, Semgrep fatal exit with parseable complete-looking path evidence, and Semgrep non-empty structured errors with exit zero. All four failed at the intended boundaries (`4 failed`): the projected command retained the source path and each three incomplete-execution payloads returned an empty finding list instead of a `tool_error`. This is the failing-before evidence for task 6.36 and authorizes only task 6.37's adapter-completion correction.

The bounded correction omits positional files only when the controller supplies the generated basedpyright project arguments; legacy no-argument invocation retains `--project .` plus explicit files. Basedpyright accepts only documented completed-analysis exit codes 0/1, while fatal/configuration/argument exits become `tool_error` and therefore UNKNOWN. Semgrep likewise accepts only completed/finding-bearing exits 0/1 and rejects malformed or non-empty top-level `errors` before path reconciliation. The four exact regressions passed together, and the complete two-runner unit surface passed `68` tests with the host CA bundle supplied for real Semgrep fixture execution. Strict OpenSpec, format, full typing (`0 errors, 0 warnings, 0 notes`), Ruff, and Pylint (`10.00/10`) passed before the release metadata update.

Release metadata now carries candidate version `0.49.58`, exact core compatibility `===0.55.1`, and unsigned filesystem checksum `sha256:77dc3a6f59e80116a1a7a7598830dd8b3648e4483789f769f1a47098ca7f0927`; the signature remains intentionally absent until the approval-time signing workflow. YAML/registry validation, bundle imports, checksum/version enforcement with missing-public-key allowance, publish precheck, `28` contract-first tests, and `git diff --check` passed. A direct complete-suite run against an isolated checkout of exact core `0.55.1` collected `1642` tests and passed `1641`; its sole failure is the already documented local macOS/CPython 3.14 capsule-lock environment gap, while the exact-core consumer smoke passed. The ordinary smart-test bootstrap deliberately reinstalled the adjacent core checkout at `0.54.0`, adding only the expected version-identity failure; the protected Linux CPython 3.11-3.13 exact-core matrix remains authoritative. The exact-core changed-line Code Review gate then returned `PASS_WITH_ADVISORY`, exit `0`, with six inherited/tool-environment warnings and no finding on a changed line.
